title: "Elder Scrolls Online Survives Xbox Reset, But ZeniMax Is Forced to Delay Its New Trial and Combat Overhaul to 2027"
---
The devastating layoffs mandated as part of Xbox's big reset hit Maryland-based developer ZeniMax Online Studios very hard, cutting 213 of its employees. Former Senior Encounter Designer Morgan Goin publicly stated that the studio couldn't possibly keep up the pace of The Elder Scrolls Online update it had strived for. Indeed, ZeniMax Online Studios swiftly told fans that the roadmap would have to change, and today Game Director Nick Giacomini has posted a letter outlining exactly how. sources:
  - source: "Wccftech"
    url: "https://wccftech.com/a-new-research-paper-claims-ai-generated-fiction-books-now-make-up-33-of-new-bestseller-entrants-crushing-human-authors-earnings"
title: "A New Research Paper Claims AI-Generated Fiction Books Now Make Up 33% Of New Bestseller Entrants, Crushing Human Authors’ Earnings"
---
It seems AI-generated slop is everywhere these days, from YouTube videos to Instagram reels. Even so, one would not ordinarily expect such low-effort content to start topping bestseller lists. And yet, this is exactly what is fast becoming a norm, with AI-generated fiction books now commanding a whopping 20% share of total sales in 2026, with a third of new entrants in bestseller lists containing AI content. Around 1 in 5 books out of the 14,419 self-published fiction ebooks were found to have substantial AI content An interesting new paper has profound implications for the growing menace of AI-generated slop. [&#8230;]

cluster_id: "20260731-0039"
published_at: "2026-07-31T02:00:03+08:00"
canonical:
  source: "IEEE Spectrum"
  url: "https://spectrum.ieee.org/ieee-publishing-ethics-research-integrity"
sources:
  - source: "IEEE Spectrum"
    url: "https://spectrum.ieee.org/ieee-publishing-ethics-research-integrity"
title: "IEEE Publishing Ethics Team Upholds Research Integrity"
---
<img src="https://spectrum.ieee.org/media-library/conceptual-illustration-of-a-stack-of-papers-impaled-onto-a-pencil-that-someone-is-using-to-mark-off-a-checkbox.jpg?id=67547233&amp;width=1245&amp;height=700&amp;coordinates=0%2C62%2C0%2C63" /><br /><br /><p>Given a rising number of <a href="https://spectrum.ieee.org/ieee-research-integrity-process" target="_self">publishing misconduct allegations</a>, <a href="https://www.ieee.org/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">IEEE</a> in 2022 created the Publishing Ethics Team as a centralized department to assist in handling claims. The group also works to increase the organization’s visibility in the broader publishing ethics area and helps IEEE volunteers write new policies.</p><p>Here are some highlights of the team’s activities last year. </p><h2>New detection tools</h2><p>IEEE conducted a pilot program in 2024 to integrate tools from the <a href="https://stm-assoc.org/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">International Association of Scientific, Technical, and Medical Publishers</a> (STM) <a href="https://stm-assoc.org/what-we-do/strategic-areas/research-integrity/integrity-hub/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Integrity Hub</a> into the peer-review workflow of <a href="https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/xpl/RecentIssue.jsp?punumber=6287639" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank"><em><em>IEEE Access</em></em></a>. The multidisciplinary, fully gold-open-access journal publishes research results across all IEEE fields of interest.</p><p>STM created the hub so scholarly publishers could access a suite of integrated, commercial, third-party research integrity tools as well as those developed by <a href="https://stm-assoc.org/what-we-do/core-services/stm-solutions/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">STM Solutions</a>. The tools help the publishing group avoid printing problematic content upon manuscript receipt, rather than reacting postpublication.</p><p>The new features include the <a href="https://stm-assoc.org/stm-integrity-hub-incorporates-clear-skies-papermill-alarm-screening-tool/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Clear Skies Papermill Alarm</a>, which helps identify potentially fraudulent manuscripts at submission. Another is an integration with the <a href="https://stm-assoc.org/integrity-hub-continues-to-gain-momentum-with-inclusion-of-pubpeer-database-in-screening-tool/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">PubPeer database</a>, which allows users to check whether references in a manuscript have received previous PubPeer comments or have been retracted—both of which can indicate quality or integrity issues. The <a href="https://stm-assoc.org/stm-integrity-hub-launches-new-research-integrity-tool/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">duplicate submissions detector</a> can determine whether the same manuscript has been submitted to multiple journals by different publishers, often a sign of academic “paper mill” activity.</p><p>Following the success of the pilot, IEEE began working last year to expand the services to all its periodicals. It is anticipated that all IEEE periodicals will be included in the Integrity Hub screening by the end of this year.</p><h2>Raising visibility</h2><p>The team participated in industry-wide initiatives with STM. It also renewed membership in groups including the <a href="https://publicationethics.org/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Committee on Publication Ethics</a>, and the team continued its work sponsoring and presenting at conferences.</p><p>At a panel presentation during the <a href="https://www.councilscienceeditors.org/annual-meeting-2025" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Council of Science Editors annual meeting</a> last year, <a href="https://www.linkedin.com/in/sulicz/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Amanda Sulicz</a>, manager of IEEE Research Integrity, participated in the Research Integrity Investigation panel session<em><em>.</em></em> She also presented at the Standardization of Publishing Integrity Norms and Corrective Actions poster session during the <a href="https://customer.sspnet.org/SSP/ssp/AM25/Home.aspx?hkey=ea26b5cc-0f12-4dc2-9401-f3be2e8148bf" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Society for Scholarly Publishing</a>’s annual meeting, held 28 to 30 May 2025.</p><p><a href="https://www.linkedin.com/in/luigi-longobardi-38390819/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Luigi Longobardi</a>, the IEEE Publishing Ethics and Conduct director, gave a presentation at the Communication and Collaboration With Institutions session during <a href="https://stm-assoc.org/events/stm-innovation-integrity-days-2025/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">STM Innovation and Integrity Days</a>, which took place 9 and 10 December.</p><p>IEEE was a sponsor of the <a href="https://sites.northwestern.edu/ncri2025/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">National Conference on Research Integrity</a>, held 20 to 22 May 2025, and the <a href="https://peerreviewcongress.org/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">International Congress on Peer Review and Scientific Publication</a>, held 3 to 5 September.</p><h2>Ethics reports</h2><p>The team is responsible for tracking <a href="https://spectrum.ieee.org/proposed-ieee-code-of-ethics-revisions" target="_self">ethics-related</a> complaints for all IEEE publications, including articles published in periodicals and conference proceedings. When complaints regarding an article’s integrity are received, either via email at <a href="mailto:pub-ethics@ieee.org" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">pub-ethics@ieee.org</a> or the anonymous <a href="https://secure.ethicspoint.com/domain/media/en/gui/20410/index.html" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">ethics reporting line</a>, the team works with IEEE volunteers to open a case, investigate the complaint, and resolve the matter.</p><p>Last year 591 cases were opened, a 56 percent increase over 2024. Of the 591 reports, 317 were closed and 274 are still under investigation. Of the complaints, 88 percent were research-related, including issues with plagiarism, AI-generated text, and falsification of—or unauthorized use of—data. The other 12 percent involved alleged misconduct by editors, reviewers, and conference organizers.</p><p>The complexity of the reported cases has expanded. An increasing number of the complaints deal with more than one article or complicated situations such as editorial misconduct or peer-review manipulation.</p><h2>Conference publications</h2><p>For the second consecutive year, the team participated in the joint <a href="https://events.ieee.org/ieee-conferences-committee/rosters-charters/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">IEEE Publication Services and Products Board/IEEE Conferences Committee’s ad hoc committee on conference publication quality</a>. The committee is tasked with analyzing and reviewing problematic conference papers and enhancing quality screening of articles prior to publication to detect integrity issues such as plagiarism and tortured phrases. The committee also updates educational modules on organizing and managing conferences.</p><p>As part of the review process, the committee focused on conference articles that contained tortured phrases, which are nonstandard English expressions that are imprecise or erroneous and give the impression of technical jargon.</p><p>Many of the articles reviewed by the ad hoc committee were identified by the <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.04895" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Problematic Paper Screener</a>, a free online tool that uses application programming interfaces to screen papers published online for potentially problematic content, such as tortured phrases, machine-generated content (<a href="https://pdos.csail.mit.edu/archive/scigen/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">SCIgen</a> or <a href="https://thatsmathematics.com/mathgen/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Mathgen</a>, for example), or references to retracted content.</p><p>The ad hoc committee was responsible for reviewing and recommending the retraction of more than 1,700 problematic conference articles last year. </p><h2>Case studies</h2><p>From the cases the team reviewed, IEEE learned valuable information to help update its publishing policies and best practices.</p><p>Here are examples of two anonymized cases reported to the team last year.</p><h3>Case Study 1</h3><br /><p><strong>Updated Policies</strong></p><p>Occasionally, a misconduct case is so complicated that it requires an update to the <a href="https://pspb.ieee.org/images/files/opsmanual.pdf" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">PSPB Operations Manual</a>.</p><p>In this particular case, Coauthor 1 reported to the Publishing Ethics Team that the work was reused in an IEEE publication without proper credit. The new work also listed two coauthors not part of the original document. Coauthor 1 also reported the case to their university’s research integrity officer (RIO) for additional investigation.</p><p>Initially, adjudicating the case proved challenging because under the PSPB policies at the time, the issue would have been classified as a multiple publication, which typically results only in a warning for the authors of the new work.</p><p>With the assistance of the RIO, it was determined that the methodological and theoretical portions of the paper were previously derived in the university’s lab; therefore, the contributions of the two new authors were not substantial enough to warrant authorship.</p><p>After deliberations by the IEEE Publishing Conduct Committee and eventually the IEEE Document Working Group, which is responsible for updates to the Operations Manual, <a href="https://www.ieee.org/publications/rights/Investigating-misconduct" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">IEEE PSPB Policy 8.2.4</a> was updated to clarify policies regarding the adjudication process for reuse of material and the proper crediting of coauthors when material has been reused.</p><h3></h3><br /><div class="horizontal-rule"></div><h3>Case Study 2</h3><br /><p><strong>Faked Reviewers</strong></p><p>Using the screening tools and data available to IEEE periodical editors, an editor in chief was alerted to suspicious reviewer activity. Specifically, two of the reviewers assigned to an article submitted the exact same review text. The editor contacted the handling editor to inform them of the irregularities and also contacted the two reviewers, asking them to verify that they were, in fact, the ones who submitted the reviewer report. Out of an abundance of caution, a new editor was assigned to the article, and new reviewers were selected while the investigation continued.</p><p>The investigation concluded that the original handling editor created and submitted both reviews in question. Following the recommendations provided to the PSPB vice president by the periodical’s Society and Publishing Conduct Committee, the handling editor was banned from publishing with IEEE and serving in an editorial capacity. </p>

title: "Ex-OpenAI researcher bets $100 billion will flow into training data because scaling alone won't cut it"
---
<p><img alt="" class="attachment-full size-full wp-post-image" height="1024" src="https://the-decoder.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/synthetic_data_illustration-2.png" style="height: auto; margin-bottom: 10px;" width="1536" /></p>
<p>        Former OpenAI employee Andrew Ho and Cambridge researcher Adam Hunt see a growing problem with large language models. Instead of becoming more versatile, the models are becoming more specialized, excelling at coding and math while stagnating or even regressing in other areas. Ho is leaving OpenAI to start a company focused on specialized training data and predicts that AI labs will need to spend more than $100 billion on targeted data collection.</p>
<p>The article <a href="https://the-decoder.com/ex-openai-researcher-bets-100-billion-will-flow-into-training-data-because-scaling-alone-wont-cut-it/">Ex-OpenAI researcher bets $100 billion will flow into training data because scaling alone won&#039;t cut it</a> appeared first on <a href="https://the-decoder.com">The Decoder</a>.</p>

The new feature reportedly reduces the latency significantly on the standard memory, eliminating any need for EXPO ULL. MSI Rolls Out High-Efficiency Mode With Four Presets to Reduce Memory Latency Without Needing AMD EXPO ULL AMD's new EXPO ULL (Ultra Low Latency) is the company's latest and enhanced EXPO memory profile that can optimize DDR5 memory timings for lower latency. This next level of optimization helps directly in memory-oriented workloads and in gaming, where applications can take advantage of tighter timings. The feature affects the sub-timings to minimize the latency on EXPO ULL memory modules, resulting in a performance boost [&#8230;]

Townfall introduces several major shifts to Silent Hill, starting with a first-person perspective and the Scottish setting of St. Amelia, an isolated island town shrouded in fog, trauma, and a troubling past. The project was first unveiled during KONAMI's October 2022 Silent Hill Transmission (which also announced the Silent Hill 2 remake).   - source: "TechOrange 科技報橘"

title: "【科技早餐】AI 算力仍供不應求：Microsoft Azure 年營收破千億美元、Meta 投資上看 1,450 億美元"
---
<p>【科技早餐】今天精選 7 則國內外重要科技新聞。 ＊Microsoft Azure 年營收首破千億，算力仍供不應求 Microsoft 最新財報顯示，2026 會計年度 Azure 營收首度突破 1,000 億美元，年增 41%；最新一季 Azure 與其他雲端服務營收也成長 43%。Microsoft 表示，企業雲端與 AI 算力需求持續超過現有供給，新增容量在當季很快轉化為用量與營收。公司最新一季在五大洲新增 31 座資料中心，全年合計新增 88 座，並增加 1 GW 運算容量，預計未來兩年讓整體容量大致增加一倍。 不過，個人電腦業務走勢明顯不同。Microsoft 最新一季 Windows OEM 與裝置營收下滑 7%，並預估新會計年度將衰退接近兩成，原因包括 PC 市場需求轉弱、零組件成本推高裝置售價、通路庫存偏高，以及去年 Windows 10 停止支援帶來的較高比較基期。Microsoft 最新一季資本支出達 410 億美元，其中約三分之二用於 CPU、GPU 等較短年限資產。這份財報顯示，大型科技公司的資源持續轉向雲端與 AI 基礎設施，企業算力與個人裝置需求也進一步分化。 ＊Meta AI 投資上看 1,450 億美元，個人代理成新商業押注 Meta 正加快把廣告業務帶來的成長，轉化為 AI 算力與資料中心投資。公司最新一季營收年增 28%，達到 [&hellip;]</p>

---
Apple stocks tumbled in late trading after component shortages weighed on the company’s sales forecast, signaling that industrywide supply constraints are taking a bigger toll than anticipated. It also gave a forecast that is weaker than expected, further hurting the stock in the after market. Bloomberg News Managing Editor for Global Consumer Tech Mark Gurman reacts to the mixed earnings report. title: "IDC’s Popal on Apple’s Earnings, Smartphone Competitors"
---
Nabila Popal, Senior Director of Data & Analytics at IDC, discusses the current state of the global smartphone market, highlighting a 7% decline in shipments in the second quarter due to extended upgrade cycles and a memory crisis impacting supply chains. Despite these headwinds, Apple has demonstrated remarkable resilience, achieving double-digit year-on-year shipment growth of 15% and strong iPhone revenue performance. She speaks with Romaine Bostick & Emily Graffeo on "The Close." (Source: Bloomberg)

sources:
  - source: "TechOrange 科技報橘"
    url: "https://techorange.com/2026/07/31/how-to-measure-ai-search-visibility"
title: "被 AI 引用不等於被推薦！AI 搜尋優化的關鍵不是可見度，而是品牌精準度"
---
<p>在 AI 搜尋快速崛起之際，許多企業開始投入「AI 可見度（AI Visibility）」優化，希望提升品牌在 ChatGPT、Google AI Overviews、Perplexity 等生成式 AI 回答中的曝光率。 數位行銷 Podcast《No Hacks》指出，目前市場最流行的 AI 可見度指標，其實很可能只是新的「虛榮指標（Vanity Metric）」。真正影響商業成果的，並非品牌被 AI 提及多少次，而是 AI 是否願意主動推薦品牌，以及 AI 對品牌本身是否建立正確且完整的認知。 AI 的「背景搜尋」正在嚴重虛胖品牌的數據 《No Hacks》指出，目前多數 AI 搜尋分析工具採用的都是「Prompt Tracking（提示詞追蹤）」模式，也就是預先設定一組問題，反覆詢問 ChatGPT、Perplexity 或 Google AI Overviews，再統計品牌出現次數。這種做法看似延續過去 SEO 關鍵字排名追蹤的邏輯，實際上卻難以反映 AI 搜尋的運作方式。技術 SEO 顧問、《No Hacks》節目來賓 Jono Alderson 指出，「這種方式有其用途，但重要性遠比大家想像得低」，真正應該思考的是如何影響 AI 對品牌的理解，而非只計算品牌是否被提及。 《No Hacks》進一步解釋，AI 系統在生成答案時，通常會自動進行多次背景搜尋（Query Fan-out），將一個 Prompt 拆解成多組查詢，再整合搜尋結果形成答案。這些背景查詢都會被 Google 計入網站曝光數，即使最終沒有任何真人點擊網站。因此，Google [&hellip;]</p>

中国推行的开源人工智能模型正在全球赢得越来越多的用户，但这种开放性也让北京感到担忧。中共担心该技术可能被黑客和犯罪分子利用，也担心模型可能会绕过审查机制。<p><img alt="习近平今年7月在上海举行的世界人工智能大会开幕式上发表讲话。他表示，中国支持人工智能开放合作，但防范失控的措施要及时完善。 Pool photo by Ng Han Guan" src="https://static01.nyt.com/images/2026/07/29/multimedia/00int-china-ai-controls-01-pljb/00int-china-ai-controls-01-pljb-articleLarge.jpg" /></p>                    <p style="text-align: right; color: gray; font-size: 11px!important; line-height: 10px;">Pool photo by Ng Han Guan</p>                    <p style="text-align: left; color: gray; font-size: 11px!important; line-height: 10px;">习近平今年7月在上海举行的世界人工智能大会开幕式上发表讲话。他表示，中国支持人工智能开放合作，但防范失控的措施要及时完善。</p>

---
The death toll from a landslide in Chongqing province, southwest China, earlier this month rose to 51, with 10 people still missing, local authorities said late on Thursday night.
The landslide, which occurred two weeks ago in Pengshui county, swept away several residential buildings at the foot of a mountain along a riverbank.


Quoting the command centre of the rescue effort, state-run news agency Xinhua said that 51 people had been found and confirmed dead, while 10 were still missing.

cluster_id: "20260731-0247"
published_at: "2026-07-31T12:24:10+08:00"
canonical:
  source: "TechOrange 科技報橘"
  url: "https://techorange.com/2026/07/31/amazon-earnings-andy-jassy-aws-win-without-top-ai-model"
sources:
  - source: "TechOrange 科技報橘"
    url: "https://techorange.com/2026/07/31/amazon-earnings-andy-jassy-aws-win-without-top-ai-model"
title: "自研模型未領先，AWS 營收卻大增 37%：Amazon 如何把多模型競賽變成平台紅利？"
---
<p>在全球 AI 賽道上，擁有最強大的自研模型，被視為科技巨頭致勝的關鍵公式，但 Amazon 卻用實際成績打破這個迷思。根據 Amazon 最新公布的 2026 年第二季財報，其雲端運算部門 AWS 繳出營收 422 億美元、年增 37% 的亮眼成績單，創下近 18 季最快增速。 對此，《Business Insider》點出一項觀察：Amazon 自家的 AI 模型目前尚未達到業界最前沿水準，因此 AWS 如今的強勁成長，很大一部分來自承載 Anthropic 與 OpenAI 等外部供應商的 AI 模型。這也呼應 Amazon 執行長 Andy Jassy 所說：「我們即使沒有自己的前沿模型，也能擁有非常成功的業務，因為未來不會由一個模型統治整個世界。」 Amazon 重整 AI 戰線，不把最強模型當唯一答案 自 2022 年 ChatGPT 問世以來，科技公司投入大量資金訓練更強大的模型，但《Business Insider》分析，這場 AI 競賽已逐漸從單純追求模型效能，轉向關注模型運行效率，以及「每一美元可取得的智慧」。 這項轉變可能對 Amazon 更為有利。相較於把打造最強自研模型放在首要位置的競爭對手，Amazon 過去從未將自研模型視為唯一考量。雖然 Amazon 曾推出 Nova 系列模型，但其能力尚未達到「最前沿」水準，因此公司近期開始大幅調整 [&hellip;]</p>

cluster_id: "20260731-0253"
published_at: "2026-07-31T12:38:22+08:00"
canonical:
  source: "TechOrange 科技報橘"
  url: "https://techorange.com/2026/07/31/anthropic-claude-incidents-cybersecurity-evaluations"
sources:
  - source: "TechOrange 科技報橘"
    url: "https://techorange.com/2026/07/31/anthropic-claude-incidents-cybersecurity-evaluations"
title: "OpenAI 才出事，Anthropic 也承認：Claude 測試曾誤駭 3 家公司"
---
<p>繼 OpenAI 上週坦承旗下多個模型在安全測試中突破隔離環境、入侵開源平台 Hugging Face 之後，Anthropic 也在美國時間 7/30 揭露一起性質相似的事件。這家 AI 公司在得知 OpenAI 事件後回頭清查自家 141,006 筆網路安全評測紀錄，發現旗下 Claude 模型自今年 4 月起，曾在 3 起獨立事件中錯誤入侵三家真實企業的系統，而其中多數受害公司當時毫無所覺。 不過不同的是，這次並非 AI 模型擺脫控制、逃離沙盒環境。Anthropic 表示，問題出在一次設定錯誤，加上多數情況下模型誤判為模擬環境，它以為攻擊真實企業，只是演習的一部分。 一次設定失誤，讓 Claude 把真實企業當成演習目標 根據 Anthropic，這三起事件都發生在名為 capture-the-flag（奪旗，CTF）的基本網路安全測試中。這類測試會給模型一個虛構情境，把一段機密資訊（flag）藏在網路上的另一台機器上，要模型設法入侵取得，過程不限定手法。Anthropic 在提示中明確告訴 Claude：這是模擬環境、沒有網路連線。但由於它與第三方評測夥伴 Irregular 之間的認知落差，測試環境實際上仍連著網路。 於是，當 Claude 在虛擬環境裡找不到目標、把搜尋範圍延伸到真實網路時，它把碰到的真實系統當成了演習的一部分。據 Anthropic 說明，Claude 使用基本的駭客技術如猜測弱密碼、進入未設身分驗證的端點等，並未動用任何複雜或未知的漏洞。 三起事件牽涉三個不同模型：Claude Opus 4.7、Claude Mythos 5，以及一個未對外發布的內部研究測試模型。這些模型在評測時未搭載對外版本會有的分類器與監控等防護，但仍保有各自的安全訓練；相關評測都跑在專用基礎設施上，與 Anthropic 的敏感內部系統及客戶資料互相隔離。 三起事件、三種模型反應 三起事件的嚴重程度與模型反應各不相同。根據 Anthropic 的說明，最嚴重的一起由 Claude Opus 4.7 [&hellip;]</p>

title: "Situational Awareness Hit by AI Selloff"
---
Situational Awareness, Leopold Aschenbrenner's hedge fund, was forced to sell billions of dollars of technology investments that had rapidly lost value, as nervous banks began to demand more collateral for his trades. Ken Griffin's Citadel hedge fund reached out to Situational Awareness and snapped up the investments at a discount, according to a person familiar with the matter. Bloomberg's Anthony Stephens reports. (Source: Bloomberg)

title: "A User’s Core i9-13900K Showed A Temperature Reading Of 155°C At Just 93W Power Draw, But There’s Little Cause For Worry"
---
A decent cooling system sometimes isn’t enough to run Intel’s Core i9-13900K at full tilt, especially knowing that these desktop processors were not only power guzzlers, but reached toasty temperatures. One user showed that the exact same CPU reached an uncomfortable 155°C when running a load of just 93W. Thankfully, here’s why you shouldn’t fret over these numbers. Monitoring software was acting buggy; the Core i9-13900K was likely showing a reading of 155°F instead of 155°C Incorrect temperature readers are some of the more common occurrences you’ll notice when firing up software like MSI Afterburner or even HWMonitor. Redditor “ertuui357” [&#8230;]

cluster_id: "20260731-0341"
published_at: "2026-07-31T16:12:19+08:00"
canonical:
  source: "Bloomberg Markets"
  url: "https://bloomberg.com/news/videos/2026-07-31/why-private-credit-got-entangled-with-insurance-video"
sources:
  - source: "Bloomberg Markets"
    url: "https://bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-07-31/why-private-credit-got-entangled-with-insurance"
  - source: "Bloomberg Markets"
    url: "https://bloomberg.com/news/videos/2026-07-31/why-private-credit-got-entangled-with-insurance-video"
title: "Why Private Credit Got Entangled With Insurance"
---
Insurers have quietly become a major driver of the private credit boom, with numerous private equity shops striking deals with insurance companies or buying them outright. But the entanglement with private credit is also changing the insurance industry itself, raising a number of questions about risk and regulation. Today we speak to Andrew Granato and Pranjal Drall, authors of a new paper, “Private Credit's State Backstop: How Private Equity Socializes Risk Through Insurers," examining the relationship between private credit and insurance. Granato (an assistant professor at the UT Austin Law School) and Drall (JD-PhD student in Financial Economics at Yale) talk to us about how PE got so interested in insurance in the first place, how both sides benefit from the relationship, and why taxpayers might ultimately be on the hook. (Source: Bloomberg)
